### P2: Ghost Cuts & Live Edit Audition (strike-through non-destructive rehearsal)

Cutting speech by text or filler finder has one universal fear: *"Did removing those 140 ums make my guest sound robotic or clip a breath?"* Today the only way to check is to commit destructive edits and Undo. Because a `DocState` is just a tiny `Vec<BlockRef>` costing zero sample copies, WavCraft can support **Ghost Cuts**:

- **Strike-through words & collapse ranges:** pressing `X` (or ticking fillers in `L4.5`) marks words or time spans as *ghosted* — struck through in the Transcript panel and dimmed with a collapse seam on the waveform — without mutating audio.
- **Zero-copy virtual playback with live D7 crossfades:** the `Player` streams a derived virtual `DocState` that skips ghosted spans with real-time 2 ms `D7` splices; pressing `G` while playing toggles **With Ghost Cuts / Uncut Original** at the playhead with zero gap (reusing `K4.9`'s Before/After engine).
- **Commit All or Discard:** one click turns kept ghost cuts into a single History step, or restores any individual word with one click even 20 minutes later.

| Step | What | Verify | Size | Kind | By | Blocked by | Issue |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P2.1 | Add `GhostSet` (sorted non-overlapping frame spans + originating word/filler tags) on `DocState`, moving with `Shift` and producing a zero-copy `DocState::without_ghosts(crossfades)` in under 2 ms on a 3-hour file. | Core test: 200 ghost cuts on a 3-hour stereo document build `without_ghosts` in `< 5 ms`, leave `D7` click-free joins on a 1 kHz sine, and survive Undo/Redo. | medium | state | script | L3.3, L4.3 |  |
| P2.2 | Stream `without_ghosts` in `Player` with gapless `G` toggle (`GHOST toggle\|commit\|clear` control verb) and strike-through rendering in the Transcript panel and waveform lane. | Headless + `tools/alpha` check: `GHOST add` strikes words without changing `frames=`, playback skips the ghosted span cleanly, `GHOST toggle` swaps uncut/cut mid-play with `underruns=0`, and `GHOST commit` pushes one History step. | medium | ui | script | P2.1, L2.4 |  |

### P3: Retake & Flub Finder + Mark While You Talk

Solo hosts and voice-over artists rarely stop recording when they stumble — they pause or clap twice and say *"let me take that sentence again from the top"*. Finding those false starts later wastes half of every edit:

- **Retake & Flub Finder (fuzzy n-gram restart detector):** scans the `Transcript` for repeated 3-to-8-word clause prefixes within a 25-second window (e.g., *"So the reason we built— sorry — So the reason we built WavCraft..."*), highlights Take 1 as a Ghost Cut candidate and keeps Take 2, with a one-click **Play Seam** audition.
- **Mark While You Talk (crash-safe live take markers):** while recording (`J1`), pressing `M` / `F8` / `Space` (or making a sharp double-clap spike followed by 1 s of silence) drops a live `.markers` entry flushed alongside `J3`'s 1-second crash-recovery header sync — opening in the editor after Stop (`J7`) as named `RETAKE` / `NOTE` markers already drawn on the `Episode Map`.

| Step | What | Verify | Size | Kind | By | Blocked by | Issue |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P3.1 | Add pure `transcript::retakes::find_retakes(&Transcript)` detecting fuzzy 3+ word restarts and self-interruptions within 25 s, returning `(flub_range, keeper_range)` pairs, surfaced alongside fillers in `L4.5` and `FILLERS retakes`. | Unit tests on 10 real-world restart patterns (exact restart, 1-word slip, *"sorry / scratch that"* bridge, legitimate rhetorical repetition excluded): 10/10 expected flub spans returned with zero false positives on clean prose. | medium | pure-fn | script | L3.1, L4.1 |  |
| P3.2 | Support live marker drops (`M` / `F8` and optional double-clap detector) during an active `Recording` (`J1`/`J3`), persisted to `<take>.recording.markers` every second and loaded into `Markers` on `finish_recording` (`J7`) and `recover_take` (`J3`). | Integration test killing a simulated recording after two live marker drops: `recover_take` restores both the audio and both markers at their exact sample frames. | medium | state | script | J1.5, J3.3, H1.3 |  |

### P4: Spectral "Find More Like This" (visual pattern spotter + batch repair)

Podcasters deal with recurring non-speech sounds that Whisper never transcribes and level detectors miss: a squeaky chair, a Slack chime, a dog bark, a lip smack or a mic bump. WavCraft already holds the whole file's spectral `Overview` in RAM (`crates/core/src/spectrogram.rs`) and feathered spectral blending (`C4.1`–`C4.3`):

- **Box one sound -> Find Similar Across File:** right-clicking any Marquee (`E`) or Lasso (`D`) spectral selection offers **Find Similar Sounds**. Normalized 2D cross-correlation over the RAM spectral overview scans a 1-hour file in ~100 ms, refines hits with full STFT cosine similarity, and pins every match on the `Episode Map` and in a review strip (`F6` / `Shift+F6` to audition, one button to feathered-attenuate or spectral-heal all ticked matches as one History step, plus an MCP tool `find_similar_sounds`).

| Step | What | Verify | Size | Kind | By | Blocked by | Issue |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P4.1 | Add `spectrogram::find_similar(doc, selection, threshold)` using coarse normalized cross-correlation on the `Overview` followed by local STFT cosine refinement, plus batch `run_effect_in_bands` applying a feathered effect across N spectral boxes in one History step. | Synthetic 10-minute speech+noise file with 5 planted 2.4 kHz notification chimes: selecting chime 1 finds all 5 within 20 ms with 0 false positives in under 100 ms, and batch -30 dB attenuate removes all 5 as one Undo step. | medium | pure-fn | script | C4.3, B2.5 |  |
| P4.2 | Add `Find Similar Sounds…` to the spectral right-click menu, `SPECTRAL similar` control verb, and `find_similar_sounds` MCP tool (`crates/automation/src/mcp.rs`). | Headless window & MCP test: marquee around chime 1 -> `SPECTRAL similar` lists 5 matches, pins them on `EPISODEMAP`, and batch attenuate is reversible by one Undo. | medium | ui | script | P4.1, C4.4, N3.2 |  |

### P5: Comfort Room Tone, Dual-Mono Guard, Natural Pause Rhythm & Show Profiles

Four small, high-craft audio intelligence touches that make edited dialogue sound human rather than chopped:

- **Auto Comfort Room Tone (smart silence):** when `D3` Silence, `L4` Shorten Pauses or `K4` Dead-Air Fix replaces a gap, optional **Comfort Room Tone** fills with seamless phase-randomized ambience synthesized from `G1`'s Noise Print (or the file's quietest 0.5 s gap) instead of jarring `-inf dBFS` digital vacuum.
- **One-Sided Stereo / Phase-Cancel Detector (`K2` + `K4`):** catches the classic podcast disaster where an interface recorded host voice onto Left only and silence onto Right (or inverted 180-degree stereo phase that vanishes on mono phone speakers), with a one-click **Convert to Mono / Fix Phase** repair.
- **Natural Pause Rhythm (`L4` upgrade):** instead of clamping every pause to a robotic flat 500 ms, scales pauses proportionally by punctuation context (~250 ms mid-clause, ~450 ms sentence end, ~750 ms topic/paragraph break).
- **One-Click Show Profiles:** saves Noise Print + Effects Rack + Match Loudness target + filler/pause rules + MP3 bitrate/mono as a single `.wcshow` profile runnable from Podcast Face (`Clean My Show`) or headlessly (`wavcraft polish --profile my-show.wcshow raw.wav -o episode.mp3`).

### P6: Verified Publish Package (Podcasting 2.0 chapters, ID3v2.4 & Raw-to-Edited Producer Map)

When an episode is ready to ship (`K5` / `M1.11`), podcasters still open a second app just to tag artwork, chapters, transcripts and show-note timestamps:

- **Embedded MP3 ID3v2.4 + `CHAP`/`CTOC` chapters from WavCraft Markers (`H1`–`H4`)**, plus sidecar **Podcasting 2.0 `chapters.json`** and **WebVTT (`.vtt`)** alongside `.srt`.
- **Raw-Time <-> Edited-Time Producer Map (`.cutmap.json`):** because `History` knows every `Shift`, WavCraft exports a bidirectional timestamp map translating notes jotted during the live recording (*"great quote at 42:15 raw"*) to their exact post-edit timestamp (*"36:08 edited"*), plus a human-readable **Episode Flight Certificate** proving post-encode decoded LUFS, true peak and zero remaining findings.

## After 1.0

Everything outside v1.md's sections A to N: multitrack sessions and the mixer, CD layout, batch processing and extra export formats, third-party plugins, video, and the remaining Audition parity rows. Their issues are on the `After 1.0` milestone with the `after-1.0` label and one `feature:` label each. Code for some of them is already on `main` (the code map marks it "present, out of v1"); M5 keeps it hidden in v1.

## Mac, PC and Linux

- **Code skeleton:** the tree the code map describes: one Cargo workspace, the app in `apps/wavcraft`, the engine in `crates/core`, the same code on all three systems.
- **Memory target:** an 8 GB Mac. All audio lives in the on-disk store; RAM holds a bounded cache; undo costs disk, not RAM (I7, [docs/v1/architecture.md](docs/v1/architecture.md)).
- **Platform-specific code:** audio output and input through `cpal` (WASAPI on Windows, CoreAudio on macOS, ALSA/PipeWire on Linux), including recording devices, their unplug handling (J5) and the macOS microphone permission prompt (J6); file dialogs through `rfd`; free disk space through `sysinfo` and `rustix`; folder access rules on Windows (`apps/wavcraft/src/win_acl.rs`). The code map's "Mac, PC and Linux" section lists every site.
- **Machines:** Windows (the build machine; no microphone, so recording is tested there with a fake input), Linux (the 64 GB Linux tablet and a SteamOS handheld in Desktop Mode; the real microphone tests), macOS on Apple Silicon (the M1 Mac mini first, then the 8 GB Mac, which is the memory target; the M1 Mac mini has 8 GB too, and I7's Mac figures were measured on it). The steps use these names: the Windows build machine, the 64 GB Linux tablet, a Linux handheld (the tablet or the SteamOS handheld), the M1 Mac mini, the 8 GB Mac.
- **CI:** `.github/workflows/check.yml` runs `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` on Linux, Windows and macOS runners, on every push to `main` and every pull request. It cannot play or record audio or open a window, which is why the hand steps exist.
- **Steps that need one system:** linux 39, mac 11, windows 26. The "By" column names it.

## Open-source pieces

In use (root `Cargo.toml`; the code map says where each is used):

- SoundCraft's engine crates, all pinned to one commit of my fork (`1435fd2`, the true-peak backport of `e3c5c67`, since #1277): `soundcraft-audio-io`, `soundcraft-dsp`, `soundcraft-model`, `soundcraft-time`, `soundcraft-mix`, `soundcraft-playback`, `soundcraft-video`, `soundcraft-vst3-host`. MIT OR Apache-2.0. Bump all together, deliberately.
- `symphonia` (MPL-2.0, decoders, unmodified), `vorbis_rs` (BSD-3-Clause; needs a C compiler), `rustfft`, `rayon`, `cpal`, `egui` / `eframe` (fonts under OFL-1.1 and the Ubuntu Font Licence), `rfd`, `arboard`, `bytemuck`, `sha2` (model hash verification, L1.7 #1557), optional `ureq` =2.12.1 behind `wavcraft-core/whisper` (`tls`, pulling `rustls` and `webpki-roots` under `CDLA-Permissive-2.0`, owner ruling 2026-11-17, L1.8 #1625), `serde`, `serde_json`, `postcard`, `dirs`, `sysinfo`, `rustix`, `getrandom`, `proptest` (tests). `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` lists all 500 third-party packages and a test fails when it misses one (I6).
- Adopted clean-room MIT modules (tracked for `PEERS.md` and `notices.py` in S5.8): `crates/core/src/transcript/align.rs` from FaderFrame `a5bb740` (L1.10a, #1628) and `crates/core/src/cut_edges.rs` (L4.8, #1197).
- My own: `craft-log` (self-logging), `craft-stage`, `wavcraft-automation`, and `craft-mp3` (the MP3 encoder, `v1.1.0`, pinned by rev to `456f049`; `save_mp3` in `crates/core/src/save.rs` calls it since A3.4, #1375).

To add for v1: the `candle` runner with the Whisper base multilingual weights (L1; the choice and the weights' licence are recorded, L0). The clean-room rule stands: no GPL, LGPL or AGPL code or dependency, and Audacity's code is never read.
